● The HTML looks right. The blank page is likely the auth guard hanging (it
  awaits waitForAuth() before the router-outlet renders anything). Let me have
  you test two things:

  1. Navigate directly to http://localhost:4200/login (unguarded route). If the
  login card renders there, the guard is stuck.
  2. Open DevTools → Application → Local Storage → http://localhost:4200 and
  clear all entries, then hard refresh (Ctrl+Shift+R). A stale Supabase session
  from the old build for a different project can make supabase.auth.getSession()
   resolve with bad data, then /api/auth/me may behave unexpectedly.

  Which do you want to try first? My guess is #2 will fix it — but #1 will
  confirm whether the guard is the cause.

  Receptionist seed grants view_order and view_report (they legitimately need
  those to see orders/bills), so TAT slipped through. Tightened to
  view_tat_dashboard | view_dashboard only — TAT is an ops/management board, not
   a reception one.

  empty. Both bottom-nav and top-strip now hide the More button when no items

  ⎿  token endpoint HTTP 400 | time 0.260018s

● Supabase itself is fine — direct curl gets a 260ms response. So the problem is
   in your browser. Most likely suspects (in order):

  1. Stale Supabase session in localStorage from an older project/build — the
  lib is trying to refresh it and hanging. You cleared localStorage but Supabase
   also writes to IndexedDB.
  2. A browser extension intercepting the supabase.co request (earlier console
  showed extension noise)

  Quick diagnostic — open an Incognito window and try http://localhost:4200.
  Incognito disables extensions and gives a clean storage. If login works there,
   the issue is extensions or cached browser storage.

  If login still hangs in Incognito, it's almost certainly supabase-js client
  state. Try this in Incognito DevTools → Application → Storage → "Clear site 
  data" (check all the boxes) → reload → try again.

  Once we know which branch it is, the fix is either (a) nothing — one-time
  cleanup, or (b) I'll add a targeted clear corrupted session on boot check in
  AuthService. Try Incognito first and tell me what happens.

● nx watch will push the update. Try again — hard-refresh the page and press
  Sign In.

  What'll happen now:
  - On app boot, the client constructor checks localStorage for any
  sb-<other-project>-auth-token. If found → purged before the Supabase client
  even initializes
  - On signIn timeout (any future hang), the service automatically signs out
  locally, wipes both localStorage and IndexedDB's supabase* databases, then
  retries signIn once. You'll see this in the console as [auth] signIn hung — 
  purging Supabase storage and retrying once

  If the first attempt failed, the silent retry should now succeed, and the
  button will only surface an error if both attempts hang — meaning the Supabase
   server genuinely isn't reachable, not a local storage corruption.

  You can keep DevTools closed — no more sticky login.

✻ Cooked for 1m 4s

> still sample collection screen is missind and where can i tell apped it to    
  frondesk or seprate in workflow                                               

● The toggle lives at Admin → Lab Settings → Sample Collection (new section at
  the top of that page).

  By default every branch boots in Front Desk mode, which intentionally hides
  the separate "Collect" sidebar item (receptionist does collection inline on
  billed orders instead). The sidebar item only appears when you flip to
  Dedicated Counter.

  Step by step:
  1. Sign in as an admin user
  2. Left nav → More → Lab settings (or URL /admin/lab-settings)
  3. Top tile: "Sample Collection" — click the Dedicated Counter radio
  4. It saves immediately (no Save button — it PATCHes on click)
  5. The nav refreshes. Users with collect_sample permission now see a "Collect"
   item in the primary nav → opens /sample-collection

  If you want reception to handle it instead: flip the radio back to Front Desk.
   The sidebar "Collect" item disappears and instead reception's Today's Orders
  shows a "Collect Sample" button on billed orders.

  If the nav item still isn't appearing after switching to Dedicated Counter:
  - Hard-refresh the page — the nav reads collectionMode from the
  BranchLabConfigService on component init
  - Check your user has the collect_sample permission. Admin/Lab Admin does by
  default; receptionist does not. If you're signed in as a receptionist, switch
  to Lab Admin or the new "Sample Collector" role

      326 +  /**                                                         
      327 +   * Flatten the sample list into one card per order so a phle
          +botomist sees                                                 
      328 +   * "patient + N vials" instead of a row per tube. Tests coun
          +t is additive                                                 
      329 +   * across tubes because one order can span departments.     
      330 +   */                                                         
      331 +  readonly groups = computed<OrderGroup[]>(() => {            
      332 +    const byOrder = new Map<string, OrderGroup>();            
      333 +    for (const s of this._pending()) {                        
      334 +      let g = byOrder.get(s.orderId);                         
      335 +      if (!g) {                                               
      336 +        const p = s.order?.patient;                           
      337 +        const meta = [                                        
      338 +          p?.sex,                                             
      339 +          p?.age != null ? `${p.age}y` : null,                
      340 +          `#${s.orderId.slice(0, 8)}`                         
      341 +        ].filter(Boolean).join(' · ');                        
      342 +        g = {                                                 
      343 +          orderId: s.orderId,                                 
      344 +          patientName: p?.displayName ?? 'Patient',           
      345 +          patientMeta: meta,                                  
      346 +          createdAt: s.createdAt,                             
      347 +          samples: [],                                        
      348 +          totalVials: 0,                                      
      349 +          collected: 0                                        
      350 +        };                                                    
      351 +        byOrder.set(s.orderId, g);                            
      352 +      }                                                       
      353 +      g.samples.push(s);                                      
      354 +      g.totalVials++;                                         
      355 +      if (s.status !== 'pending') g.collected++;              
      356 +    }                                                         
      357 +    return Array.from(byOrder.values())                       
      358 +      .sort((a, b) => a.createdAt.localeCompare(b.createdAt));
      359 +  });

● Rebuild clean and HMR pushed. Hard-refresh /sample-collection (or
  /lab/collect) to see the new UI.

  What you get now:

  - Top scan strip: big barcode icon, single input that accepts either a scan
  gun's keystrokes or you typing the code manually + Find button. Same flow the
  lab tech uses.
  - Pending queue: one card per patient/order (not per tube). Each card shows:
    - A row of coloured vial pills — lavender for Haematology, gold for
  Biochemistry, red for Serology, green for Microbiology, grey for
  Histopathology
    - Patient name, sex · age · order #
    - "N vials" badge; if any are already drawn, a "done" badge shows 2/3 done
  - Click the card → it expands in place, showing one tube card per vial:
    - Vertical coloured stripe matching the department's tube type
    - Tube label ("EDTA · Lavender", "SST · Gold", etc.)
    - The printed barcode
    - Tests in that tube
    - Mark collected / Can't collect buttons (the reject prompt now says
  "patient refused, vein not found, wrong tube…" to speed the phlebotomist)
  - Collected tubes visually dim with a green tick — so the collector sees at a
  glance which vials still need drawing
  - Deep link: /sample-collection?orderId=xxx auto-expands that order — that's
  what reception's "Collect Sample" button uses

  Hard-refresh and open a billed order to see it.

● Three new defences pushed. When nx rebuilds and you hard-refresh, the sign-in
  flow is now:

  1. Try supabase-js signInWithPassword (6 s limit)
  2. On timeout → purge storage + retry SDK once
  3. Still timeout → fallback direct POST to /auth/v1/token (completely bypasses
   the SDK), then hand the session back to the SDK via setSession()

  I also disabled Supabase Realtime (we don't use it) and routed all SDK fetches
   through a cache: 'no-store' wrapper. Realtime is a common hang culprit — it
  opens a WebSocket on init and some SDK versions block auth calls waiting on
  it.

  After this, if signing in still hangs, the cause is network-level (extension
  blocking *.supabase.co, or a DNS/proxy problem) — and the error message will
  now name the specific failing step so we can home in. Hard-refresh and try.

● Build clean, HMR pushed. Hard-refresh /sample-collection.

  New flow:

  1. Tap any patient card → full-screen drawer slides in from the right (bottom
  sheet on mobile)
  2. The drawer shows the patient, a live progress bar (0/3), and each vial as a
   tap-able card with its tube color
  3. Tap the vial body → fills a big green check circle, marks it collected on
  the backend. No separate "Mark collected" button — the whole card is the tap
  target.
  4. Each tap shows the optimistic tick instantly, then confirms with server
  state
  5. When the last vial is tapped, you get a toast "Sita Raman — all 3 vials 
  collected" and the drawer auto-closes
  6. "Done" button at the bottom is disabled until every vial is drawn
  7. "Can't collect" in the footer opens a reason prompt and flags all remaining
   pending vials for re-collection

  The close (×) + clicking the backdrop + Escape (once the browser has focus)
  all exit the drawer.

● Clean. Hard-refresh, tap a patient.

  New look:

  - Full-screen stage, not a side drawer. Purple + teal radial glow backdrop,
  heavy blur, fade-and-zoom entry animation
  - Centered header: large gradient patient name, monospace meta (sex · age ·
  order #) underneath, a dedicated progress card on the right showing 0 / 3 with
   glowy tracker bar spanning the width
  - Vial cards are now centered portraits instead of rows:
    - The vial is much bigger (84×260), dominant on its card
    - Each card has a subtle glow in the tube's colour that brightens on hover,
  and lifts on hover with a bloom shadow
    - Label + department code (monospace, coloured to match tube) + barcode +
  tests all centered below
    - Status pill at the bottom — "Tap when drawn" (muted) or "✓ Collected ·
  14:32" (green pill)
  - Done tubes get a green-tinted border and glow
  - Footer: ghost "Can't collect" on the left, live counter / done badge in the
  middle, primary "Done" on the right (disabled until everything's drawn)
  - Mobile-responsive — single column, smaller vial, tightened padding

● Clean rebuild, HMR pushed. Hard-refresh and tap a patient.

  Redesign highlights:

  Vial itself — now a real vacutainer:
  - Colored rubber stopper with proper light/shadow gradient across the cap
  - Rim groove where stopper meets glass (the dark line every real tube has)
  - Hemispherical rounded bottom (not a flat box)
  - Thin vertical highlight streak + subtle right-side shadow give it glass
  depth
  - Small volume tick marks on the right side
  - Blood fill has a meniscus at the top and a dark pool gradient toward the
  base
  - Collection tick pops with a scale-bounce animation

  Card:
  - Dropped the huge Code128 + QR barcode block — it was swallowing the card.
  Replaced with a small monospace code number below the name, which is all a
  phlebotomist actually needs to cross-check against the label the scanner
  already printed
  - Tube name is primary (big), department code is a tiny coloured all-caps
  monospace under it (like a category tag)
  - First test name inline with "+N" chip for the rest (tooltip shows the full
  list on hover)
  - Time-of-collection pill at the bottom when done, "Tap when drawn" pill
  otherwise
  - Card gets a coloured top-down glow that matches the tube — very subtle in
  default state, lifts on hover

✻ Baked for 1m 59s

● Addressing both: the light-mode styles are in, and now fixing the disappearing
   vial bug.

  The bug: after PATCH /collect succeeds, I was re-fetching
  /api/samples/pending-collection, which filters out already-collected samples.
  So the tapped vial evaporated instead of sitting there with blood + tick. Fix:
   keep the status update purely local while the drawer is open, and only
  refresh from the server when the drawer closes.

  - Backdrop: softer lilac-teal wash on a near-white base (rgba(246, 247, 253,

  for light contrast
  - Headings gradient inverts to slate-to-black instead of white-to-gray

  Disappearing vial — tapping a vial now just flips its status locally +
  PATCHes; no re-fetch while the drawer is open, so the just-drawn vial stays
  visible with its fill and tick. When every vial is in, there's a small 700 ms
  pause so you see the last fill finish, then the drawer closes and a refresh
  drops the order from the outer queue. If the PATCH fails, the local status
  rolls back so you can retry.

● Clean rebuild. Hard-refresh and pick a patient whose order includes a urine
  test (Urinalysis, urine culture, etc.).

  How containers are now resolved:
  The first test's sampleType on each sample decides what you see:

  - Urine* → amber urine cup with yellow screw-cap lid (wider, squatter), fill
  is amber yellow
  - Blood - EDTA → lavender-capped vacutainer
  - Blood - SST / Gold → gold cap
  - Blood - Plain / Red → red cap
  - Blood - Fluoride / Grey → grey cap
  - Blood - Citrate / Blue → blue cap
  - Blood - Heparin / Green → green cap
  - Stool / Sputum / Swab → green-capped sterile container (falls back to
  vacutainer SVG for now)
  - Anything unrecognised → falls back to the conventional tube colour for that
  department

  Tap behaviour is identical — the cup drains/fills just like a vial. Down the
  road we can add distinct SVGs for stool/swab if you want, but this covers the
  urine case you asked about.

● Clean rebuild and HMR pushed. Hard-refresh.

  The fix: when the drawer opens, its root element is detached from the
  component's template view and appended to <body> directly. That bypasses the
  .shell-stack stacking context entirely, so the drawer now paints above the top
   nav (and everything else). When it closes, the element is removed cleanly.

  Angular's view bindings (click handlers, @if state, signal updates) all still
  work because we're only moving the DOM node, not detaching the rendered view.
  The component's encapsulation attributes ride with the node, so the CSS still
  applies.

  You should now see:
  - Full-viewport dark backdrop (top nav disappears behind it)
  - Patient name + meta + progress card centered at the very top
  - Vials genuinely centered horizontally; footer pinned to the real bottom of
  the screen

● Clean rebuild. Hard-refresh.

  What changed:

  Scan strip — now the clear primary CTA on the page:
  - Big teal-tinted band with rounded edges and a soft shadow
  - Large barcode icon on the left, "SCAN OR ENTER BARCODE" small-caps label
  above the input field, monospace placeholder PF-AP-2026-00001-HAEM
  - Dedicated teal Find button on the right instead of the generic button
  - Focused input has no visible border; it reads like a labelled input you just
   type into

  Patient cards — redesigned so two adjacent cards don't look like column
  dividers:
  - Avatar on the left (the first-initial circle used elsewhere in the app)
  - Patient name + monospace meta in the middle — both truncate instead of
  wrapping
  - Rack of coloured chips to the right of the name, sitting inside a small dark
   "holder" (so the tube pills don't float at the card edge)
  - Purple count pill + right-arrow chevron on the far right — the chevron makes
   "tap to open" explicit
  - On hover: subtle violet wash, the chevron slides right 2 px and turns white

  Grid — minmax(380px, 1fr) at the page level with 12 px gaps. On your 1920
  screen you'll see 3 columns, on 1280 two, on mobile one. The avatar + chevron
  establish clear per-card boundaries so no more column-seam illusion.

● Clean rebuild, HMR pushed. Hard-refresh and sign in.

  The change: sign-in no longer routes through supabase.auth.signInWithPassword.
   That was the reliable hang source — the SDK spins up its realtime WebSocket
  and a Web-Locks-based refresh pipeline on cold starts that can stall before
  the actual auth POST goes out.

  New flow (total worst case ~14s, typical ~400ms):
  1. Direct POST
  https://cottlnsbjnstsgknsgzu.supabase.co/auth/v1/token?grant_type=password — 6
   s AbortController timeout. Your curl showed 260ms so this should be
  essentially instant.
  2. setSession on the SDK so realtime/refresh still function. 2 s timeout;
  non-fatal if it stalls — we already have the access token.
  3. /api/auth/me against your backend — 6 s timeout.

  The console will now log [auth] directTokenFetch NNN ms and [auth] loadProfile
   NNN ms so you can see the real timing. If this still times out, the direct
  fetch itself is being blocked — and the error message will clearly point at
  *.supabase.co being unreachable (extension/firewall), which is a network-level
   problem we can't fix in code.

● Clean rebuild. Hard-refresh and sign in.

  Root cause found: the 6s wasn't the /api/auth/me HTTP call — it was
  supabase.auth.getSession() inside our getAccessToken(). The SDK's getSession
  hangs on cold starts (the same Web-Locks issue that made signInWithPassword
  hang). Backend itself answers an authenticated request in ~450ms.

   — which we populated directly from directTokenFetch. The SDK call is only a
  fallback, and it's capped at 1.5s so it can't hang either.

  Your console should now log:
  - [auth] directTokenFetch ~300ms
  - [auth] loadProfile ~500ms

● Clean rebuild. Hard-refresh.

  What I hardened against overlap:

  - Cards now have position: relative; overflow: hidden; box-sizing: border-box
  — any inner content that grows too wide (very long patient name, 10+ vials in
  the rack, a hover translate) is clipped to the card instead of bleeding into
  its grid neighbour.
  - Rack is capped at max-width: 120px with its own overflow: hidden — orders
  with a lot of samples no longer push the count pill off the right edge.
  - Chips shrink-hardened with flex-shrink: 0 (they never got below their
  declared 5 px width).
  - Chevron hover translate removed — that 2 px slide could visually cross into
  the next card on hover; colour-only feedback now.
  - Grid minimum bumped to 400 px so column breaks happen where there's
  genuinely enough room.

  If there's still a specific spot where two tiles overlap after the refresh —
  drop a screenshot and I'll zero in on it.

● Already seeded and live in the DB.

  - Role: Sample Collector (pre-seeded, IsPreSeeded: true)
  - Role ID: 0c223f6f-f211-4db7-96d0-94193b79dc08
  - Permissions attached:
    - view_patient — See patient records in the queue
    - view_order — See the order + test list behind a vial
    - collect_sample — Mark a vial as drawn (the toggle action)
    - reject_sample — Flag "can't collect" with a reason
    - print_barcode — Reprint vial labels if the original smudges

  This role:
  - Drives the "Collect" sidebar nav item (only shown when collection_mode = 
  dedicated_counter AND user holds collect_sample)
  - Gates the /sample-collection route (blocked by
  permissionGuard('collect_sample'))
  - Does not include admin/settings permissions, so a Sample Collector can't
  access tests, users, branches, or billing config — only the collection queue

  To assign it: Admin → Users & Access → Roles & Permissions — the role appears
  in the list as "Sample Collector" (Pre-seeded). Invite a user and pick "Sample
   Collector" in the role picker on the invite screen.

  ⎿  Running in the background (↓ to manage)

● Backend restarting with the notification dispatch. I'll verify in ~60s.

  Behaviour:

  Every time a billed order is created, the system checks the branch's
  collection_mode. If it's dedicated_counter, every active user holding the 
  "Sample Collector" role who's scoped to that branch (or tenant-wide) gets an
  in-app notification row inserted:

  {
    "type": "lms.sample_ready_collection",
    "title": "New sample to collect — Sita Raman",
    "body":  "Order #a1b2c3d4 · Chennai Main",
    "referenceId": "<order id>",
    "referenceType": "order"
  }

  That row is picked up by the existing notification bell in the top strip
  (which polls via NotificationsController and shows an unread count). Clicking
  the bell shows the list; referenceId + referenceType will let us route the
  collector directly into /sample-collection when they tap the notification (the
   bell component already has that pattern for other event types).

  Failure in the notification step is logged but non-fatal — order creation
  always succeeds even if the notify stage fails.

  How it all flows together now:

  1. Billed order created in a branch whose collection_mode = dedicated_counter
  2. OrderService.NotifyCollectorsAsync runs. It reads the tenant's
  NotificationConfig rows.
  3. If the admin has sample_ready_collection set to InAppEnabled = false, the
  fan-out is skipped entirely — no notifications ever created.
  4. If it's true (default), each active user with the Sample Collector role
  scoped to that branch gets a notification row: type: 
  lms.sample_ready_collection, title "New sample to collect — {patient}", linked
   to the order.
  5. The user's notification bell picks it up on its next poll.

  Admin control:
  - Admin → Notifications → Alerts tab now shows Sample ready for collection
  alongside the other events, with In-App / WhatsApp / Email toggles. Flipping
  In-App off stops the fan-out immediately on the next order.
  - Everything is stored in the tenant's notification_configs table, so the
  toggle survives restarts and applies to every branch in the tenant.

  The principle is also saved to memory so any future notification I add (lab
  pickup alerts, pathologist validations, etc.) will go through the same gate —
  never firing unconditionally.

      35 +    public async Task SupersedeReferenceAsync(Guid tenantId, st
         +ring referenceType, Guid referenceId, Guid claimantUserId, stri
         +ng claimantName)                                               
      36 +    {                                                          
      37 +        // Only touch unread notifications — once a collector h
         +as dismissed a                                                 
      38 +        // notification we don't want to rewrite what they saw.
      39 +        var rows = await _db.Notifications                     
      40 +            .Where(n => n.TenantId == tenantId                 
      41 +                     && n.ReferenceType == referenceType       
      42 +                     && n.ReferenceId == referenceId           
      43 +                     && !n.IsRead                              
      44 +                     && n.RecipientId != claimantUserId)       
      45 +            .ToListAsync();                                    
      46 +        if (rows.Count == 0) return;                           
      47 +                                                               
      48 +        var now = DateTime.UtcNow;                             
      49 +        var stamp = string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(claimantName) ? "
         +another user" : claimantName;                                  
      50 +        foreach (var n in rows)                                
      51 +        {                                                      
      52 +            n.IsRead = true;                                   
      53 +            n.ReadAt = now;                                    
      54 +            n.Body = $"Picked up by {stamp}";                  
      55 +        }                                                      
      56 +        await _db.SaveChangesAsync();                          
      57 +    }                                                          
      58 +}

● Bash(cd /c/Users/Shuganth/Documents/GitHub/prefrontal-mw/Prefrontal &&
      ConnectionStrings__Default='Host=aws-1-ap-northeast-1.pooler.supabase.com;
      Port=5432;Database=p…)
  ⎿  Running in the background (↓ to manage)

● Backend restarting with the new supersede behaviour. In ~60s it'll be up.

  How it works now:

  When Sample Collector A taps the first vial on Sita Raman's order:
  1. PATCH /api/samples/{id}/collect runs as before (vial marked collected,
  blood fills in A's UI)
  2. Right after, NotificationRepository.SupersedeReferenceAsync rewrites every
  OTHER collector's still-unread sample_ready_collection notification for that
  same order:
    - IsRead = true → their bell's unread count drops by 1
    - Body = "Picked up by <A's display name>" → when they glance at their
  notification list they see it was claimed, not dropped

  Edge cases handled:
  - Already-read notifications are never rewritten — if collector B had already
  dismissed the ping, nothing changes; their memory of it is preserved.
  - Idempotent — when A taps the 2nd/3rd vial, SupersedeReferenceAsync finds
  nothing unread to rewrite and is a no-op.
  - Non-fatal — if the supersede DB update fails, the actual collect still
  succeeds; we swallow the error and log.
  - A's own notification is untouched by the claimant filter (RecipientId != 
  claimantUserId) so if A wants to re-open the drawer from their own bell, that
  path still works.

  The claimant's display name comes from _user.Name (which UserContextMiddleware
   populates from UserEntity.DisplayName). Falls back to "another user" if
  somehow empty.
